ETA is 1h40m. That's the same speed as single connection - the proxy is throttling per-IP, not per-connection.\\n\\nTotal bandwidth through the proxy is hard-capped at ~150 KiB/s regardless of connections. At this rate:\\n- 1 model: ~1h40m\\n- 9 models (serial): ~15 hours\\n- 9 models (5 parallel): still limited by total bandwidth = ~3h each = 6h total\\n\\nThis is fundamentally impractical. I need a completely different strategy.\\n\\n\\nLet me explore alternatives: checking if ModelScope has a Chinese mirror, deriving Jaccard bounds from the paper's aggregate metrics without per-example data, or using statistical estimation based on accuracy and sample size to compute theoretical Jaccard under independence assumptions.\\n\\nActually, approach #4 is really promising—if I have accuracy values for each seed, I can calculate the expected Jaccard using the formula J = p_i * p_j / (p_i + p_j - p_i * p_j), and the actual Jaccard would be higher if examples tend to be consistently easy or hard across seeds. I can pull the accuracy values from the polypythias-evals dataset and compare against these theoretical bounds.\\n\\nThe challenge is that computing correct-set Jaccard, helped/hurt Jaccard, and ambiguity alpha all require knowing which specific examples each seed gets right, which I don't have without the per-example data. But I can still estimate these under an independence assumption—for instance, the expected correct-set Jaccard would be approximately p²/(2p - p²) where p is the accuracy.\\n\\nFor 410M Pythia on LAMBADA with accuracy around 0.60-0.65, this gives an expected Jaccard of about 0.43 under independence. But the actual Jaccard is much higher because seeds share most of their correct and incorrect examples, and that gap between actual and theoretical is what reveals the consistency pattern.\\n\\nSince I really need the actual values rather than just theoretical estimates, the most practical approach is to download 3 models and run the analysis directly. Three seeds give me three pairwise comparisons, which should be enough for a preliminary calibration result, and I can focus specifically on LAMBADA and ARC-Easy.\\n\\nAt 50 KiB/s per model with parallel downloads, each would take about 5 hours—still too slow. But wait, I just realized the earlier snapshot_download calls were surprisingly fast, completing in 15-20 seconds. The issue is they only grabbed the small files, not the full 869MB weight file. The hf_hub_download got stuck, but maybe loading directly through transformers' from_pretrained with the proxy set would use a different code path that's faster.\\n\\nActually, I'm noticing a pattern here. The proxy seems to have two different speeds: API calls and small file downloads are quick (seconds), but large files from the xet bridge CDN are throttled to around 150 KB/s. This throttling might be per-connection, per-IP, or per-destination. If it's destination-based, I could try downloading from a different endpoint entirely. Let me explore whether there's a legacy HF storage URL or if git clone would work better for pulling the model This means the CDN is overwhelmed or blocking us because of too many connections/requests.\\n\\nThe multi-connection approach is being actively blocked by the CDN. The CDN returns 503 when too many concurrent connections hit it.\\n\\nOK so the fundamental issue is:\\n1. Single connection: too slow (~150 KB/s, ~1.5h per model)\\n2. Multi connection: CDN returns 503 or corrupts files\\n\\n\\n3. Maybe I should try a middle ground with just 2-3 connections per file, or accept the slower speed and download a subset of models instead of all 9.\\n\\nWait, I need to reconsider this. The first run with 16 connections actually did download the files successfully—they were the correct size at 911MB—but they appeared corrupted. The files started with the PK header which is valid for ZIP files, yet zipfile rejected them. I should double-check whether these are actually ZIP files or a different format, since PyTorch models created with `torch.save` should be ZIPs.\\n\\nLooking at the bytes more carefully, PK\\\\x03\\\\x04 is definitely the ZIP local file header, so the format is correct. The real problem is \\\"failed finding central directory\\\"—the file's end is missing the central directory records that ZIP files require. This points to a proxy or CDN issue where multi-connection downloads are corrupting segments of the file.\\n\\nLet me try one more approach: use Python's `requests` library with streaming instead. It uses a single connection but might handle the proxy better than wget or curl. Though looking back at the speeds, wget was only getting around 100KB/s on large files, while the earlier `snapshot_download` for seed1 finished in 15-20 seconds despite downloading 83MB total.\\n\\nWait, that's actually 5.5 MB/s—much faster. So `snapshot_download` reported success but didn't actually download the weight file. I think the issue is that when `HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1` is set and the model uses xet storage, `snapshot_download` skips the large file while caching the small ones directly. Let me test without that environment variable At 150KB/s per model:\\n\\n\\n- Serial: 3 × 1.5h = 4.5h\\n- Parallel (2-3): ~2-2.5h\\n\\nBut wait, I'm realizing the proxy actually supports higher throughput when I use multiple parallel connections. Earlier aria2c with 5 parallel downloads was hitting ~650KB/s total, which means if I download 3 models simultaneously with single connections each, I'd get around 200KB/s per model. status_forcelist=[500, 502, 503, 504])\\nadapter = HTTPAdapter(max_retries=retry)\\nsession.mount(\\\"https://\\\", adapter)\\nsession.mount(\\\"http://\\\", adapter)\\n\\ndef download_file(url, dest, chunk_size=1024*1024):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Download with resume support.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    downloaded = 0\\n    headers = {}\\n    if os.path.exists(dest):\\n        downloaded = os.path.getsize(dest)\\n        headers[\\\"Range\\\"] = f\\\"bytes={downloaded}-\\\"\\n    \\n    for attempt in range(5):\\n        try:\\n            r = session.get(url, headers=headers, stream=True, timeout=120, proxies=PROXY)\\n            if r.status_code == 416:  # Range not satisfiable = already complete\\n                return True\\n            if r.status_code not in (200, 206):\\n                print(f\\\"  HTTP {r.status_code}\\\", flush=True)\\n                time.sleep(10)\\n                continue\\n            \\n            total = int(r.headers.get(\\\"Content-Length\\\", 0)) + downloaded\\n            mode = \\\"ab\\\" if r.status_code == 206 else \\\"wb\\\"\\n            if r.status_code == 200:\\n                downloaded = 0\\n            \\n            with open(dest, mode) as f:\\n                for chunk in r.iter_content(chunk_size=chunk_size):\\n                    f.write(chunk)\\n                    downloaded += len(chunk)\\n            \\n            print(f\\\"  Downloaded {downloaded/1024/1024:.1f}MB\\\", flush=True)\\n            return True\\n            \\n        except Exception as e:\\n            print(f\\\"  Attempt {attempt+1} failed: {e}\\\", flush=True)\\n            time.sleep(15 * (attempt + 1))\\n            headers[\\\"Range\\\"] = f\\\"bytes={os.path.getsize(dest)}-\\\" if os.path.getsize(weight_file) > 50000000:\\n        # Quick validity check\\n        import zipfile\\n        try:\\n            z = zipfile.ZipFile(weight_file)\\n            z.close()\\n \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/pythia_weights_70m\\\"\\n\\nsession = requests.Session()\\nretry = Retry(total=5, backoff_factor=5)\\nadapter = HTTPAdapter(max_retries=retry)\\nsession.mount(\\\"https://\\\", adapter)\\n\\ndef download_weight(model_id, dest):\\n    url = f\\\"https://huggingface.co/{model_id}/resolve/main/pytorch_model.bin\\\"\\n    \\n    for attempt in range(10):\\n        downloaded = try:\\n            r = session.get(url, headers=headers, stream=True, timeout=(30, 60), proxies=PROXY)\\n            \\n            if r.status_code == 416:\\n                return True\\n    last_report = time.time()\\n                for chunk in r.iter_content(chunk_size=131072):\\n                    f.write(chunk)\\n                    downloaded += len(chunk)\\n                    if time.time() - last_report > 30:\\n                        pct = downloaded / total * 100 if total else 0\\n                        print(f\\\"    {downloaded/1024/1024:.1f}/{total/1024/1024:.1f}MB ({pct:.0f}%)\\\", flush=True)\\n                        last_report = time.time()\\n            \\n            print(f\\\"    Complete: {downloaded/1024/1024:.1f}MB\\\", flush=True)\\n            return True\\n            \\n        except Exception as e:\\n            print(f\\\"    Attempt {attempt+1} error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\\\", flush=True)\\n            time.sleep(15 * (attempt + 1))\\n    \\n    return False\\n\\n# Main\\nprint(f\\\"=== START {time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')} ===\\\", flush=True)\\n\\nfor seed in SEEDS:\\n    model_id = f\\\"EleutherAI/pythia-70m-seed{seed}\\\"\\n true, \\\\\\\"data\\\\\\\": {\\\\\\\"stdout\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"E4 Pythia Seed Calibration Analysis\\\\\\\\nComputes Jaccard overlap of correct/helped/hurt sets across seeds.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\nimport json, os, itertools, sys\\\\\\\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\\\\\\\nimport numpy as np\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nbase = Path(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"/root/entrainment_gated_ft/results/e4_pythia_seed/\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef load_samples(seed_dir, task_prefix):\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Load per-example results from lm-eval log_samples files.\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n    seed_dir = Path(seed_dir)\\\\\\\\n    # lm-eval 0.4.x saves samples as samples_&lt;task>_*.jsonl\\\\\\\\n    for f in sorted(seed_dir.rglob(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"samples_{task_prefix}*.jsonl\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")):\\\\\\\\n        results = {}\\\\\\\\n        with open(f) as fh:\\\\\\\\n            for line in fh:\\\\\\\\n                doc = json.loads(line)\\\\\\\\n                doc_id = doc.get(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"doc_id\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", doc.get(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"idx\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"))\\\\\\\\n                # lm-eval stores per-metric results; check common acc fields\\\\\\\\n                acc = doc.get(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"acc\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", doc.get(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"exact_match\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"))\\\\\\\\n                if acc is None:\\\\\\\\n                    # For some tasks, check filtered metrics\\\\\\\\n                    for k, v in doc.items():\\\\\\\\n                        if \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"acc\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" in k.lower() and isinstance(v, (int, float)):\\\\\\\\n                            acc = v\\\\\\\\n                            break\\\\\\\\n                if acc is not None:\\\\\\\\n                    results[doc_id] = int(acc)\\\\\\\\n        if results:\\\\\\\\n            return results\\\\\\\\n    return None\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef jaccard(a, b):\\\\\\\\n    if not a and not b:\\\\\\\\n        return 1.0\\\\\\\\n    return len(a & b) / len(a | b)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# Collect results\\\\\\\\ntasks = [\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"lambada_openai\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"arc_easy\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]\\\\\\\\nseed_dirs = sorted(base.glob(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"seed*\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"))\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nif not seed_dirs:\\\\\\\\n    print(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"ERROR: No seed directories found\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    sys.exit(1)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nprint(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Found {len(seed_dirs)} seed directories: {[d.name for d in seed_dirs]}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nanalysis = {}\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nfor task in tasks:\\\\\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n{'='*60}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Task: {task}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"{'='*60}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\n    # Load all seed results\\\\\\\\n    seed_results = {}\\\\\\\\n    for sd in seed_dirs:\\\\\\\\n        res = load_samples(sd, task)\\\\\\\\n        if res is not None:\\\\\\\\n            correct_set = {k for k, v in res.items() if v == 1}\\\\\\\\n            total = len(res)\\\\\\\\n            seed_results[sd.name] = {\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"correct\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\": correct_set, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"total\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\": total, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"acc\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\": len(correct_set)/total}\\\\\\\\n            print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"  {sd.name}: {len(correct_set)}/{total} correct ({len(correct_set)/total:.4f})\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\n    if len(seed_results) < 2:\\\\\\\\n        print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"  Need at least 2 seeds, got {len(seed_results)}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n        continue\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\n    seed_names = sorted(seed_results.keys())\\\\\\\\n    n = len(seed_names)\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\n    # 1. Correct set Jaccard (all pairs)\\\\\\\\n    j_correct = []\\\\\\\\n    for i, j in itertools.combinations(range(n), 2):\\\\\\\\n        si, sj = seed_names[i], seed_names[j]\\\\\\\\n        j_val = jaccard(seed_results[si][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"correct\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], seed_results[sj][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"correct\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"])\\\\\\\\n        j_correct.append(j_val)\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n  Correct-set Jaccard (all {len(j_correct)} pairs):\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"    mean={np.mean(j_correct):.4f}, std={np.std(j_correct):.4f}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"    min={min(j_correct):.4f}, max={max(j_correct):.4f}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n    \\\\\\\\n    # 2.
